% Document Purpose
\chapter*{Document Purpose and Usage}
\addcontentsline{toc}{chapter}{Document Purpose and Usage}

This catalog provides a comprehensive overview of thruster requirements per Bureau Veritas Rules for Classification (NR467) and related guidance, designed as a practical reference for plan approval surveyors.

\section*{Key Features}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*]
    \item Complete drawing and document lists for all thruster types
    \item Explicit approval categories (\fa{} or \fic{})
    \item Cross-references for overlapping requirements (steering gear, main shafting, hydraulics)
    \item Surveyor checklists by thruster type
    \item Testing and commissioning requirements
    \item Common submission errors and review tips
\end{itemize}

\section*{Document Structure}
The catalog is organized into the following chapters:
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*]
    \item \textbf{The Rule-Mandated Submissions (Authoritative)} --- the verbatim rule tables
    \item General Requirements (All Thrusters $\geq$110kW)
    \item Transverse Thrusters (Tunnel \& Retractable)
    \item Rotating and Azimuth Thrusters (incl. Podded Units)
    \item Water-Jet Propulsion Systems
    \item Voith-Schneider (Cycloidal) Propulsion Systems
    \item Other Thruster Types (Rim-Driven, Pump-Jet, Gill/Jet, Steering Grid)
    \item Prime Mover Considerations --- Diesel-Engine vs. Electric-Motor Driven Thrusters
    \item Thrusters as Part of Steering Gear
    \item Thrusters as Part of Main Shafting
    \item Hydraulic Systems \& Pitch Control
    \item Dynamic Positioning (DP) Thrusters
    \item Ice Class \& Cold-Climate (Winterisation) Requirements
    \item Additional BV Class Notations Affecting Thruster Packages
    \item Ship-Type \& Service-Notation Specific Requirements
    \item Testing \& Commissioning
    \item Surveyor Checklists by Type
    \item Submission Cover Sheets by Ship Type (one-page tick-off)
    \item Common Submission Errors
    \item Quick Reference Tables
    \item Regulatory References
    \item Contact Information
\end{enumerate}

\section*{How to Use This Catalog (Surveyor Workflow)}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*]
    \item \textbf{Identify the thruster type} (Chapters \ref{ch:transverse}--\ref{ch:othertypes}) and confirm rated power. Thrusters $\geq$110~kW attract the full Sec.\,15 submission set.
    \item \textbf{Identify the prime mover} --- electric motor or diesel engine (Chapter \ref{ch:primemover}) --- and add the corresponding drive-train drawings.
    \item \textbf{Identify the functional role(s)}: propulsion, steering (Chapter \ref{ch:steering}), main shaft line (Chapter \ref{ch:shafting}), or dynamic positioning (Chapter \ref{ch:dp}). Each role \emph{adds} to the base package.
    \item \textbf{Overlay additional notations \& service constraints}: ice class / winterisation (Chapter \ref{ch:ice}), other BV additional class notations (Chapter \ref{ch:notations}), and the ship-type / service-notation requirements incl.\ SRtP, hazardous-area and offshore rules (Chapter \ref{ch:shiptype}).
    \item \textbf{Verify hydraulics/pitch} (Chapter \ref{ch:hydraulics}) and \textbf{testing \& commissioning} (Chapter \ref{ch:testing}).
    \item \textbf{Run the type-specific checklist} (Chapter \ref{ch:checklists}) and screen against common errors (Chapter \ref{ch:errors}).
\end{enumerate}

\section*{Navigating this PDF}
This document is fully hyperlinked for quick reference:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*]
    \item \textbf{Bookmarks panel} --- open your PDF reader's bookmarks/outline panel to jump to any chapter or section.
    \item \textbf{Table of contents} --- every entry is clickable.
    \item \textbf{Cross-references} --- ``Chapter~X'', ``Section~Y'' and table references in the text are clickable links.
    \item \textbf{Search} --- the text is fully selectable and searchable (e.g.\ search ``MON-SHAFT'', ``escort tug'', ``ice'').
\end{itemize}

\section*{Legend}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*]
    \item \fa{} = \textbf{For Approval} — corresponds to the rule's category \textbf{A} (``to be submitted for approval'')
    \item \fic{} = \textbf{For Information} — corresponds to the rule's category \textbf{I} (``to be submitted for information'')
    \item \textbf{During Construction} = Certificates/reports generated during manufacturing
\end{itemize}

\vspace{0.3cm}
\noindent\fcolorbox{warningred}{lightgray}{\begin{minipage}{0.95\textwidth}
\small\textbf{\textcolor{warningred}{How to read this catalog --- two tiers of content}}

\smallskip
\noindent\textbf{Tier 1 --- what the rule actually demands.} Chapter
\ref{ch:ruletables} reproduces the \emph{verbatim} submission tables of NR467
Sec.\,15, Sec.\,14, Part F and NR584, with the rule's own \textbf{A}/\textbf{I}
categorisation and an exact citation for every row. \textbf{That chapter is the
authority. If anything elsewhere in this catalog conflicts with it, Chapter
\ref{ch:ruletables} governs.}

\smallskip
\noindent\textbf{Tier 2 --- what a surveyor examines to approve those items.}
Chapters \ref{ch:transverse}--\ref{ch:othertypes} and the discipline chapters
set out the engineering content behind each rule item: the calculations,
drawings and analyses that a reviewer normally needs in order to be satisfied
that (say) ``Propeller'' or ``Rotating mechanism of the thruster'' can be
approved. \textbf{This is professional practice, not a rule-mandated document
list.} A client cannot be required to submit a Tier 2 line as a separate
deliverable purely because it appears here --- cite the Tier 1 row it supports.
\end{minipage}}

\vspace{0.3cm}
\noindent\textit{Note on rule references:} Thruster requirements are found in \textbf{NR467, Part C, Chapter 1, Section 15} (``Thrusters''); steering gear in \textbf{Section 14}; shaft lines and propellers in \textbf{Sections 7--8}. Bracketed sub-references such as [2.3] are structural pointers within this catalog. Ice class, comfort, automation and DP requirements sit in \textbf{NR467 Part F} (Additional Class Notations, e.g.\ DYNAPOS for DP); service notations are in \textbf{Part D} (general) and \textbf{Part E} (offshore service vessels \& tugs). Note \textbf{NR216} is the Materials \& Welding rule note (not a DP rule). Always confirm the exact article against the rule edition applicable to the vessel's contract/build date.

\newpage

% Chapter: THE RULE TABLES (authoritative)
\chapter{The Rule-Mandated Submissions (Authoritative)}
\label{ch:ruletables}

This chapter reproduces the \emph{actual} submission tables of the rules, with
the rule's own \textbf{A}/\textbf{I} categorisation and an exact citation for
every row. It was transcribed from Bureau Veritas Rules Explorer against
\textbf{NR467 edition Jul 2026} and \textbf{NR584 edition Apr 2026}.

\noindent\fcolorbox{warningred}{lightgray}{\begin{minipage}{0.95\textwidth}
\small\textbf{\textcolor{warningred}{This chapter is the authority.}} Where any
other chapter of this catalog lists a document that does not appear here, that
document is \emph{supporting technical content} (see the two-tier note in the
Document Purpose chapter) --- not a rule-mandated submission. Always confirm
the exact article against the rule edition applicable to the vessel's contract
date at \url{https://rulesexplorer.bureauveritas.com/}.
\end{minipage}}

\section{Scope, definitions and thresholds}
\label{sec:scope}

\subsection{Power threshold}
NR467 Pt C, Ch.\,1, Sec.\,15, [1.1.1] applies to thrusters developing power
\textbf{equal to 110\,kW or more}. A unit rated at exactly 110\,kW \emph{is}
in scope. Below that, [1.1.2]: thrusters of less than 110\,kW are to be built in
accordance with sound marine practice and tested as required in [3.2] to the
satisfaction of the Surveyor --- the Sec.\,15 plan tables are not applied.

\subsection{What is (and is not) a thruster}
\textbf{Sec.\,15 [1.2.1]:} ``A thruster is a propeller installed in a
\emph{revolving} nozzle or in a special transverse tunnel in the ship, or a
water-jet. A thruster may be intended for propulsion, manoeuvring and steering
or any combination thereof. \textbf{Propulsion propellers in fixed nozzles are
not considered thrusters} (see Ch.\,1, Sec.\,8, [1.1.1]).''

\vspace{2mm}
\noindent\fcolorbox{warningred}{white}{\begin{minipage}{0.95\textwidth}
\small\textbf{\textcolor{warningred}{Scope trap:}} a conventional propulsion
propeller inside a \emph{fixed} Kort nozzle is \textbf{not} a thruster. It is
assessed under Sec.\,8 (Propellers), not Sec.\,15. Confirm whether the nozzle
revolves before applying this catalog.
\end{minipage}}

\vspace{2mm}
\noindent Other definitions: \textbf{[1.2.2]} a \emph{transverse thruster} is an
athwartship thruster developing thrust in a transverse direction for manoeuvring
purposes; \textbf{[1.2.3]} an \emph{azimuth thruster} can rotate through
360\textdegree{} to develop thrust in any direction; \textbf{[1.2.4]} a
\emph{water-jet} is a tubular casing (duct) enclosing an impeller, which may have
means for deviating the jet to provide a steering function.

\subsection{Number of units}
\textbf{Sec.\,15 [1.3.1]:} ``In general, \textbf{at least two azimuth thrusters}
are to be fitted in ships where these are the sole means of propulsion. Single
azimuth thruster installations will be specially considered by the Society on a
case by case basis.'' This requirement \emph{also applies to water-jets}.

\subsection{Which tables apply}
Per Sec.\,15 [1.4]: athwartship and azimuth thrusters $\rightarrow$
\textbf{Tab.\,1 + Tab.\,2 + Tab.\,3}; water-jets $\rightarrow$ \textbf{Tab.\,4};
manufacturer data $\rightarrow$ \textbf{Tab.\,5}, submitted together with the plans.

\subsection{Duty changes the technical rule set}
\begin{longtable}{|p{3.6cm}|p{5.4cm}|p{5.4cm}|}
\caption{Manoeuvring vs.\ propulsion --- rule consequences} \label{tab:dutysplit} \\
\hline
\rowcolor{bvblue}
\textcolor{white}{\textbf{Aspect}} & \textcolor{white}{\textbf{Manoeuvring only}} & \textcolor{white}{\textbf{Propulsion / steering}} \\
\hline
\endfirsthead
\hline
\rowcolor{bvblue}
\textcolor{white}{\textbf{Aspect}} & \textcolor{white}{\textbf{Manoeuvring only}} & \textcolor{white}{\textbf{Propulsion / steering}} \\
\hline
\endhead
\hline
\endlastfoot
Propeller [2.2.2] & Sec.\,8 [2.5] \textbf{without} the 10\% thickness increase & Sec.\,8 [2.5] in full \\
\hline
Shaft [2.2.3] & Formula in Sec.\,15 [2.2.3]; $+$10\% in way of a keyed connection & Ch.\,1, Sec.\,7, [2.2.3] \\
\hline
Gears [2.2.4] & Sec.\,6 with \textbf{auxiliary} gear safety factors & Sec.\,6 with \textbf{propulsion} gear safety factors \\
\hline
Material tests & \textbf{Need not} be witnessed by a Surveyor, provided full test reports are made available --- [3.1.2] & \textbf{In the presence of a Surveyor}, per Sec.\,8 [4.1] --- [3.1.1] \\
\hline
Ice class & \textbf{Only} Pt F, Ch.\,8, Sec.\,3, [3.5.1] (tunnel grids) & Full Pt F, Ch.\,8 \\
\hline
Steering gear & Not applicable & Sec.\,14 applies, incl.\ Article 4 \\
\hline
\end{longtable}

\section{Calculations demanded by the Sec.\,15 design articles}

These are not in the tables, but the rule text of Article 2 expressly requires
them to be submitted.

\begin{longtable}{|p{4.6cm}|p{1.1cm}|p{7.8cm}|}
\caption{Submissions required by the text of NR467 Sec.\,15, Article 2} \label{tab:s15calc} \\
\hline
\rowcolor{bvblue}
\textcolor{white}{\textbf{Reference}} & \textcolor{white}{\textbf{A/I}} & \textcolor{white}{\textbf{Requirement}} \\
\hline
\endfirsthead
\hline
\rowcolor{bvblue}
\textcolor{white}{\textbf{Reference}} & \textcolor{white}{\textbf{A/I}} & \textcolor{white}{\textbf{Requirement}} \\
\hline
\endhead
\hline
\endlastfoot
Sec.\,15 [2.2.5] & \fa{} & Nozzle structure per \textbf{Part B, Chapter 12}. Nozzle-to-hull scantlings and weld type/size specially considered; the Society \emph{reserves the right to require a detailed stress analysis} for certain high power installations. \\
\hline
Sec.\,15 [2.2.5] & \fa{} & For \textbf{steerable} thrusters, the \textbf{equivalent rudder stock diameter} is to be calculated in accordance with Part B, Chapter 12. \\
\hline
Sec.\,15 [2.2.6] & --- & Transverse thruster tunnel thickness \textbf{not less than the adjacent part of the hull}. Tunnels connected to the hull by devices other than welding are specially considered. \\
\hline
Sec.\,15 [2.3.2] & \fa{} & Water-jet guide vanes and shaft supports: \textbf{``Fatigue strength calculation is to be submitted.''} \\
\hline
Sec.\,15 [2.3.3] & \fa{} & Water-jet stator/rotor: no natural frequency in the vicinity of the excitation frequencies due to stator--rotor hydrodynamic interaction. \textbf{``Calculations are to be submitted for maximum speed and any currently used speed.''} \\
\hline
Sec.\,15 [2.3.5] & --- & Water-jet steering performance and emergency steering availability at least \textbf{equivalent to Sec.\,14 [4.2] and [4.3]}. \\
\hline
Sec.\,15 [2.4.1] & --- & Steering controls from the \textbf{navigation bridge, the machinery control station and locally}; means to stop any running thruster at each control station; a \textbf{thruster angle indicator at each steering control station, independent of the control system}. \\
\hline
\end{longtable}

\textcolor{white}{\textbf{Identification of system parameter}} & \textcolor{white}{\textbf{Alarm}} \\
\hline
Steering oil pressure & Low \\
\hline
Oil tank level & Low \\
\hline
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\noindent These two lines are the \emph{minimum} alarm and monitoring
requirements the rule sets for propulsion and steering thrusters (see also
Sec.\,14 [4]). Anything beyond them is either an automation-notation
requirement (Part F, Ch.\,3) or good practice --- not Sec.\,15.

\subsection{Sec.\,14 Article 4 --- performance criteria a surveyor must verify}
\label{sec:s14art4}

Article 4 is titled ``Design and construction --- Requirements for ships equipped
with thrusters as steering means''. Its numeric criteria are the heart of the
steering review:

\begin{longtable}{|p{2.6cm}|p{12.3cm}|}
\caption{NR467 Sec.\,14, Article 4 --- criteria} \label{tab:s14crit} \\
\hline
\rowcolor{bvblue}
\textcolor{white}{\textbf{Reference}} & \textcolor{white}{\textbf{Criterion}} \\
\hline
\endfirsthead
\hline
\rowcolor{bvblue}
\textcolor{white}{\textbf{Reference}} & \textcolor{white}{\textbf{Criterion}} \\
\hline
\endhead
\hline
\endlastfoot
[4.1.1] & The main and auxiliary steering gear may consist of \textbf{azimuth thrusters, water-jets or cycloidal propellers} complying with Sec.\,15 as far as applicable. \\
\hline
[4.1.2] & Where the steering means consists of \textbf{two or more thrusters}, the control system is to include a device ensuring \textbf{automatic synchronisation of the thruster rotation}, unless each thruster is designed to withstand any additional forces resulting from the thrust exerted by the other thrusters. \\
\hline
[4.2.2] & \textbf{Multiple} steering-propulsion units: each unit to have a main and an auxiliary steering gear, or two or more identical steering actuating systems per [4.2.7]. Failure of one shall not render the other inoperative. \\
\hline
[4.2.3] & \textbf{Single} steering-propulsion unit: two or more steering actuating systems per [4.2.7], and \textbf{a detailed risk assessment is to be submitted} demonstrating that on any single failure in the steering gear, control system and power supply, ship steering is maintained. \\
\hline
[4.2.5] & \textbf{Main} steering: adequate strength and capable of steering at maximum ahead service speed (to be demonstrated); capable of changing direction from one side to the other at the declared steering angle limits at an average turning speed of \textbf{not less than 2,3\textdegree/s} with the ship running ahead at maximum ahead service speed; \textbf{power operated for all ships}; so designed as not to be damaged at maximum astern speed (need not be proved by trials). \\
\hline
[4.2.6] & \textbf{Auxiliary} steering: capable of steering at navigable speed and of being brought speedily into action; average turning speed \textbf{not less than 0,5\textdegree/s} with the ship running ahead at one half of maximum ahead service speed \textbf{or 7 knots, whichever is the greater}; power operated where necessary to meet \textbf{SOLAS II-1/29.4.2} and in any ship having \textbf{more than 2\,500\,kW propulsion power per steering-propulsion unit}. \\
\hline
[4.2.7] & Auxiliary steering gear may be \textbf{omitted} where the main steering gear has two or more identical power units and actuators and: on a \emph{passenger ship} satisfies [4.2.5] with any one power unit out of operation; on a \emph{cargo ship} satisfies [4.2.5] operating with all power units; and after a single failure in piping or one power unit, steering capability is maintained or speedily regained. Electric steering motors count as part of the power unit and actuator. \\
\hline
[4.2.8] & Where propulsion power \textbf{exceeds 2\,500\,kW per thruster unit}, an alternative power supply is to be provided \textbf{automatically within 45\,s}, from the emergency source or an independent source located in the steering gear compartment (used only for this purpose), sufficient for the steering arrangements, their control system and the steering gear response indicator. Capacity \textbf{at least 30\,min} for ships of 10\,000\,GT and upwards, \textbf{at least 10\,min} for any other ship. \\
\hline
[4.3.1] & The use of \textbf{water-jets as steering means will be given special consideration} by the Society. \\
\hline
\end{longtable}

\noindent Declared steering angle limits are declared by the directional control
system manufacturer for each ship-specific non-traditional steering means.
Manoeuvrability tests (IMO Res.\ MSC.137(76)) are to be carried out at steering
angles not exceeding those limits.

\newpage

\section{Electric drive --- NR467 Pt C, Ch.\,2}
\label{sec:electricrule}

Sec.\,15 [2.2.1] routes the drive as follows: \textbf{diesel engines}
$\rightarrow$ Ch.\,1, Sec.\,2; \textbf{electric motors and their feeding
systems} $\rightarrow$ \textbf{Ch.\,2, Sec.\,4}. Two express provisions apply to
electric drives:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*]
\item provisions are to be made to \textbf{prevent starting of the motors
whenever there are insufficient generators in operation};
\item \textbf{intermittent duty thrusters} will be the subject of special
consideration by the Society.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Motors above 1\,MW}
\textbf{NR467 Pt C, Ch.\,2, Sec.\,4, Article 5} --- ``Additional tests for
rotating machines used as propulsion motor or thruster''. In addition to the
standard Tab.\,1 tests, machines developing \textbf{more than 1\,MW} are subject
to the following during their assembly:

\rowcolor{bvblue}
\textcolor{white}{\textbf{Item}} & \textcolor{white}{\textbf{Requirement / test}} \\
\hline
\endfirsthead
\hline
\rowcolor{bvblue}
\textcolor{white}{\textbf{Item}} & \textcolor{white}{\textbf{Requirement / test}} \\
\hline

\hline

Shaft line & Requirements of Ch.\,1, Sec.\,7 apply \\
\hline
Rotor winding assembly & Dynamic balancing \\
\hline
Stator winding assembly & Dielectric test (after impregnation); insulation resistance measurement (after impregnation) \\
\hline
Frame & Visual examination in compliance with design drawings; \textbf{liquid penetrant test of 10\% of the structure welds and 100\% of the handling points} \\
\hline
Watercooler & Visual examination in compliance with design drawings; performance test (temperature-rise measurement) \\
\hline
Hydrostatic jacking unit & Pressure test; working test under nominal conditions \\

\section{Dynamic positioning --- NR467 Pt F, Ch.\,11, Sec.\,5 (DYNAPOS)}
\label{sec:dpruletable}

Sec.\,15 [1.1.1]: ``For azimuth thrusters intended for dynamic positioning, the
additional requirements in \textbf{Pt F, Ch.\,11, Sec.\,5} are to be complied
with.'' DYNAPOS [1.1.3] cross-refers back to Pt C, Ch.\,1, Sec.\,2 (engine-driven
thrusters), \textbf{Pt C, Ch.\,1, Sec.\,15} (azimuthal and transverse thrusters)
and Pt F, Chapter 3 (automation).

only''}, and [2.4.1] confirms: ``For installation intended to be assigned the
notation \textbf{DYNAPOS AM/AT R} and \textbf{DYNAPOS AM/AT RS} an FMEA is to be

attract an FMEA.} Do not demand one as a matter of course.

\section{Ice class --- NR467 Pt F, Ch.\,8}
\label{sec:iceruletable}

\subsection{The notations}
Pt F, Ch.\,8, Sec.\,1, [1.1.1] assigns: \textbf{ICE CLASS IA SUPER, ICE CLASS
IA, ICE CLASS IB, ICE CLASS IC, ICE CLASS ID, YOUNG ICE 1, YOUNG ICE 2}. Per
[1.1.2], the strengthening requirements --- \emph{excepting} ICE CLASS ID,
YOUNG ICE 1 and YOUNG ICE 2 --- are equivalent to the \textbf{Finnish-Swedish
Ice Class Rules 2019, as amended}, for ships trading in the Baltic in winter or
equivalent ice conditions.

\subsection{The scope split --- this is where packages are most often over-demanded}

\vspace{1mm}
\noindent\fcolorbox{warningred}{lightgray}{\begin{minipage}{0.95\textwidth}
\small\textbf{\textcolor{warningred}{NR467 Sec.\,15 [1.1.1], verbatim:}}
``Thrusters intended for propulsion and steering of ships with an ICE CLASS
notation are to comply with the additional requirements of Part F, Chapter 8.
\textbf{Transverse thrusters intended for manoeuvring of ships with an ICE CLASS
notation are required to comply with additional requirement Pt F, Ch.\,8,
Sec.\,3, [3.5.1] only.}''

\smallskip
\noindent And \textbf{Pt F, Ch.\,8, Sec.\,3, [3.5.1] in its entirety} reads:

\smallskip
\noindent\textit{``Tunnels of transverse thrusters are to be fitted with grids
for protection against ice impacts.''}

\smallskip
\noindent\textbf{That is the whole ice requirement for a manoeuvring bow or
stern tunnel thruster on an ice-class ship: a protective grid.} Do not apply
the propulsion-machinery ice package to it.
\end{minipage}}

\subsection{Propulsion and steering thrusters --- the full package}
Pt F, Ch.\,8, Sec.\,3, Article 1 applies to propulsion machinery for ICE CLASS
IA SUPER / IA / IB / IC. [1.1.1] states that the regulations ``\emph{also apply
to azimuthing and fixed thrusters for main propulsion, considering loads
resulting from propeller-ice interaction and loads on the thruster body/ice
interaction}''.

\begin{longtable}{|p{2.6cm}|p{12.3cm}|}
\caption{Pt F, Ch.\,8, Sec.\,3, Article 1 --- propulsion machinery in ice} \label{tab:iceart1} \\

\hline
\endfirsthead
\hline
\rowcolor{bvblue}

\hline

\section{NR584 --- Azimuth thrusters in ice for polar class ships and icebreakers}
\label{sec:nr584}

\noindent\fcolorbox{warningred}{lightgray}{\begin{minipage}{0.95\textwidth}
\small\textbf{\textcolor{warningred}{A separate rule note --- easily missed.}}
\textbf{NR584} (edition Apr 2026) applies to ships fitted with an \textbf{azimuth
propulsion system} and assigned a service notation \textbf{icebreaker} or an
additional class notation \textbf{POLAR CLASS}. It is a complete rule note in its
own right, with 8 sections and its own submission tables, and it is
substantially more demanding than NR467 Sec.\,15. If your vessel is polar-class
with azimuth propulsion, \emph{this} is the controlling document.
\end{minipage}}

\subsection{Application and structure}
Per NR584 Sec.\,1, [1.1.2] the azimuth propulsion system may be:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*]
\item a \textbf{podded electrical thruster} --- steering unit, slewing bearing,
rudder part of the system, and the pod containing the electric motor; or
\item a \textbf{Z/L geared thruster} --- steering unit, upper gearing (Z-drive),
lower gearing, nozzle, and the prime mover (diesel engine or electric motor).
\end{itemize}

\noindent The eight sections are: Sec.\,1 General; Sec.\,2 Structure of the
Thruster Body; Sec.\,3 Machinery Assessment; Sec.\,4 Electrical Installations;
Sec.\,5 Steering Units; \textbf{Sec.\,6 Structural Assessment of the Thruster
Body in Ice}; Sec.\,7 Tests; Sec.\,8 Certification.

\subsection{Submission tables}
NR584 Sec.\,1, [1.3] requires \textbf{Table 2} (55 items, below), plus
\textbf{Table 3} for solid propellers \emph{or} \textbf{Table 4} for built-up and
controllable pitch propellers, plus \textbf{Table 5} manufacturer data.

% Chapter 1: General Requirements
\chapter{General Requirements - All Thrusters $\geq$110kW}
\label{ch:general}

\section{Applicability}
All thrusters developing power equal to 110 kW or more ($\geq$110 kW) must comply with NR467 Section 15 requirements (NR467 Pt C, Ch.1, Sec.15, [1.1.1]). Thrusters of less than 110 kW are to be built in accordance with sound marine practice and tested per Sec.15 [3.2] to the satisfaction of the Surveyor (NR467 Pt C, Ch.1, Sec.15, [1.1.2]) --- verify with surveyor for the exact reduced scope.

\section{Two Axes to Classify Before Listing Drawings}
\label{sec:duty-config}

Before applying any type checklist, fix two attributes of the unit --- they change
\emph{which} rules and drawings apply as much as the thruster type does:

\subsection{Duty: manoeuvring vs.\ propulsion}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*]
\item \textbf{Manoeuvring (auxiliary)} --- e.g.\ a bow/stern tunnel thruster used
only for low-speed manoeuvring. Assessed under Sec.\,15 as auxiliary machinery;
it is \emph{not} part of the main shaft line.
\item \textbf{Propulsion (main)} --- e.g.\ an azimuth on a tug, a podded unit, a
water-jet or a Voith-Schneider that propels the ship. The propeller, shaft and
gear are classed as \textbf{main propulsion}: the main-shaft-line requirements of
Chapter \ref{ch:shafting} apply, a \textbf{torsional vibration calculation is
mandatory}, and barred/continuous-speed ranges must be confirmed.
\item \textbf{Take-me-home / emergency propulsion} --- a retractable or auxiliary
unit providing get-home capability; assess independence from the main train
(cross-reference redundant-propulsion notation, Chapter \ref{ch:notations}).
\end{itemize}
The same physical thruster therefore attracts a heavier package when it is the
ship's propulsion than when it is purely for manoeuvring.

\subsection{Propeller configuration: open vs.\ ducted (nozzle)}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*]
\item \textbf{Open (non-ducted) propeller} --- blade and hub strength, cavitation
and the usual shaft/bearing package.
\item \textbf{Ducted (nozzle) propeller} --- \emph{adds} the nozzle/duct structure
and strength drawing, nozzle profile/geometry with propeller-tip clearance, and
the nozzle-to-unit/hull connection. Distinguish:
  \begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Fixed nozzle} (Kort-type) --- structural item only; improves
  bollard pull.
  \item \textbf{Steering nozzle} --- the nozzle also steers, so the
  \textbf{steering-gear package} of Chapter \ref{ch:steering} applies (actuator,
  control, redundancy, SOLAS II-1/29 where it is the ship's means of steering).
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\noindent\textbf{Surveyor note:} record duty and propeller configuration on the
submission cover sheet (Chapter \ref{ch:coversheets}). The interactive
Quick-Reference tool builds the exact drawing list once type, duty, propeller
configuration, prime mover, role, ship type and notations are selected.

% Chapter 2: Transverse Thrusters
\chapter{Transverse Thrusters (Tunnel \& Retractable)}
\label{ch:transverse}

\noindent\fcolorbox{warningred}{lightgray}{\begin{minipage}{0.95\textwidth}
\small\textbf{\textcolor{warningred}{Rule basis for this chapter.}} The
rule-mandated submissions for a transverse thruster are \textbf{Sec.\,15
Tab.\,1} (Table \ref{tab:s15t1}, 5 rows) $+$ \textbf{Tab.\,2} (Table
\ref{tab:s15t2}, 4 rows) $+$ \textbf{Tab.\,5} (Table \ref{tab:s15t5}, 4 rows)
--- \textbf{13 rows in total}. Everything below expands those rows into the
engineering content a reviewer examines. It is \emph{supporting practice}, not
an independent list of deliverables a client can be required to produce.

\smallskip
\noindent Note also \textbf{Sec.\,15 [2.2.6]}: the tunnel thickness is not to be
less than the adjacent part of the hull; and for a manoeuvring-only unit the
propeller need not carry the 10\% thickness increase, the shaft follows the
Sec.\,15 [2.2.3] formula, and gears use \emph{auxiliary} gear safety factors.
\end{minipage}}

% Chapter 3: Azimuth Thrusters
\chapter{Rotating and Azimuth Thrusters}
\label{ch:azimuth}

\noindent\fcolorbox{warningred}{lightgray}{\begin{minipage}{0.95\textwidth}
\small\textbf{\textcolor{warningred}{Rule basis for this chapter.}} The
rule-mandated submissions for a rotating or azimuth thruster are \textbf{Sec.\,15
Tab.\,1} (Table \ref{tab:s15t1}) $+$ \textbf{Tab.\,3} (Table \ref{tab:s15t3})
$+$ \textbf{Tab.\,5} (Table \ref{tab:s15t5}) --- \textbf{14 rows in total}. If
the unit is also the ship's steering means, add the Sec.\,14 Tab.\,1 item 13
subset (Section \ref{sec:s14thruster}) and verify the Article 4 criteria
(Section \ref{sec:s14art4}). Everything below is the supporting engineering
content behind those rows.

\smallskip
\noindent \textbf{Nozzle and hull connection --- Sec.\,15 [2.2.5]:} the nozzle
structure is assessed under \textbf{Part B, Chapter 12}; nozzle-to-hull
scantlings and weld type/size are specially considered and the Society may
require a detailed stress analysis for certain high power installations. For
\emph{steerable} thrusters the \textbf{equivalent rudder stock diameter} is
calculated per Part B, Chapter 12.

\smallskip
\noindent \textbf{If POLAR CLASS or icebreaker:} \textbf{NR584} governs --- see
Section \ref{sec:nr584}.
\end{minipage}}

% Chapter 4: Water-Jets
\chapter{Water-Jet Propulsion Systems}

\noindent\fcolorbox{warningred}{lightgray}{\begin{minipage}{0.95\textwidth}
\small\textbf{\textcolor{warningred}{Rule basis for this chapter.}} Water-jets
have their \emph{own} table: \textbf{Sec.\,15 Tab.\,4} (Table \ref{tab:s15t4},
7 rows), submitted per [1.4.2], plus \textbf{Tab.\,5} manufacturer data (Table
\ref{tab:s15t5}). Note that Tab.\,4 places the \emph{general arrangement} and
the \emph{sealing gland} in category \fic{}.

\smallskip
\noindent Two calculations are demanded by the rule text itself:
\textbf{[2.3.2]} a \emph{fatigue strength calculation} for guide vanes and shaft
supports; and \textbf{[2.3.3]} a \emph{stator/rotor blade natural-frequency
calculation} for maximum speed and any currently used speed, demonstrating no
natural frequency near the stator--rotor hydrodynamic interaction excitation.

\smallskip
\noindent \textbf{[2.3.5]}: steering performance and emergency steering
availability at least equivalent to Sec.\,14 [4.2] and [4.3]. Note Sec.\,14
[4.3.1]: \emph{the use of water-jets as steering means will be given special
consideration by the Society}. \textbf{[1.3.1]} (at least two units where they
are the sole means of propulsion) applies to water-jets as well.
\end{minipage}}
\label{ch:waterjets}

% Chapter: Voith-Schneider / Cycloidal
\chapter{Voith-Schneider (Cycloidal) Propulsion Systems}

\noindent\fcolorbox{bvblue}{white}{\begin{minipage}{0.95\textwidth}
\small\textbf{Rule basis.} NR467 Sec.\,15 does not define a cycloidal
propeller, so there is no dedicated table. The rule does, however, expressly
recognise cycloidal propellers as an acceptable steering means:
\textbf{Sec.\,14 [4.1.1]} --- ``The main and auxiliary steering gear \ldots{} may
consist of thrusters of the following types: azimuth thrusters, water-jets,
\textbf{cycloidal propellers}, complying with the provisions of Ch.\,1, Sec.\,15,
\emph{as far as applicable}.'' Apply the rotating/azimuth tables (Tab.\,1 $+$
Tab.\,3 $+$ Tab.\,5) as the closest fit and \textbf{agree the scope with the
Society in writing before review}.
\end{minipage}}
\label{ch:vsp}

\section{Description \& Classification Basis}

The Voith-Schneider Propeller (VSP), or cycloidal propeller, is a vertical-axis
propulsor in which a set of vertical blades projects below a rotating casing
(rotor). Each blade oscillates about its own vertical axis through a kinematic
control gear linked to a movable control (steering) centre. By displacing the
control centre, the unit produces thrust of \emph{any magnitude in any
horizontal direction} at constant rotor speed. A VSP therefore combines
\textbf{propulsion and steering in a single unit}; the steering function makes
\textbf{NR467 Part C, Ch 1, Sec 14 (Steering Gear) and SOLAS II-1 Reg.\,29
requirements applicable} (see Chapter \ref{ch:steering}). Typical applications:
Voith Water Tractors, double-ended ferries, buoy tenders, mine-countermeasure
vessels.

VSP units are assessed under \textbf{NR467 Pt C, Ch 1, Sec 15} for the
mechanical thruster elements, \textbf{Sec 14} for the steering/control function,
and \textbf{Sec 7} where the drive forms part of the propulsion shaft line.

\noindent\textbf{Surveyor note (VSP):} A VSP is simultaneously a propeller, a
gear unit \emph{and} a steering gear. Do not approve the mechanical package in
isolation --- confirm that the steering-function documentation of
Chapter \ref{ch:steering} (redundancy, control stations, alarms, response time,
SOLAS compliance) has also been submitted. The blade guard/bottom plate and its
hull attachment are frequently under-documented; require structural
substantiation against grounding loads.

\newpage

% Chapter: Other Thruster Types
\chapter{Other Thruster Types (Rim-Driven, Pump-Jet, Gill/Jet, Steering Grid)}

\noindent\fcolorbox{warningred}{lightgray}{\begin{minipage}{0.95\textwidth}
\small\textbf{\textcolor{warningred}{No dedicated rule tables exist for these
types.}} NR467 Sec.\,15 [1.2] defines only three things: the \emph{transverse
thruster}, the \emph{azimuth thruster} and the \emph{water-jet}. The unit types
in this chapter must be mapped onto the closest applicable table --- rim-driven
and steering-grid units onto Tab.\,3 (rotating/azimuth), pump-jet and gill/jet
units onto Tab.\,4 (water-jet) --- and \textbf{the scope agreed with the Society
in writing before review}. Do not present the lists below as rule requirements.

\smallskip
\noindent Remember the scope exclusion of \textbf{[1.2.1]}: a propulsion
propeller in a \emph{fixed} nozzle is \textbf{not} a thruster and is assessed
under Sec.\,8.
\end{minipage}}
\label{ch:othertypes}

This chapter addresses less-common propulsor arrangements recognised by BV. Each
follows the \textbf{general submissions of Chapter \ref{ch:general}} plus the
requirements of the \emph{nearest analogous conventional type}, with the
additional type-specific items below. Confirm the intended notation and service
(propulsion, take-me-home, DP, steering) before fixing the package.

\section{Rim-Driven Thrusters (Integrated Motor Propulsors)}

Rim-driven / hubless thrusters integrate a permanent-magnet (or induction) motor
whose rotor forms the propeller-blade tip ring inside the duct. There is no
mechanical shaft line or reduction gear; excitation and speed are set by a
frequency converter. Assessment shifts from gearing toward
\textbf{electrical machine, converter and duct-bearing} substantiation.

\begin{longtable}{|p{0.5cm}|p{5cm}|p{1.5cm}|p{5.5cm}|p{2cm}|}

\item Fatigue and corrosion allowance
\item Cavitation assessment
\end{itemize} & NR467 Sec.15, [6.1]; Sec.7 \\
\hline

\end{longtable}

\section{Pump-Jet \& Ducted Rotor-Stator Propulsors}

Pump-jet units (ducted rotor with stator vanes, often for low-signature or
shallow-draught craft) are treated as a ducted propeller + shaft line. Submit the
\textbf{duct/stator structure and strength, rotor blade design and strength,
shaft line (Chapter \ref{ch:shafting}), bearings, seals} and the relevant drive
package. Where the unit also steers (deflector/vectoring), add the steering
function package of Chapter \ref{ch:steering}.

\section{Gill / Hydro-Jet Transverse Thrusters (Pump-Type)}

Pump-type transverse thrusters draw water through a hull inlet and discharge
through athwartship outlets, providing side thrust without an open tunnel. Submit
as for a tunnel thruster (Chapter \ref{ch:transverse}) but replace the tunnel
structure items with: \textbf{inlet grating and duct, internal pump/impeller
design and strength, discharge outlet and directional-valve arrangement, hull
penetration and watertight integrity}, plus the pump drive package.

\section{Steering Grid / Steering-Nozzle \& Rudder-Propeller Variants}

Fixed-nozzle (Kort-type) units with a steering grid or a steerable nozzle behave
as combined propulsion + steering devices. Submit the \textbf{nozzle/grid
structure and strength, propeller in nozzle, shaft line, steering actuator and
control}, and the full steering-gear compliance package of
Chapter \ref{ch:steering} (SOLAS II-1 Reg.29 where the device is the ship's
means of steering).

\noindent\textbf{Surveyor note (other types):} For any novel or hybrid
propulsor, apply the \emph{function-based} approach --- decompose the unit into
propeller/impeller, structure, shaft line, gear (if any), drive
(electric/diesel/hydraulic), bearings/seals and control --- and require the
corresponding chapter's submissions for each function. Novel technology may
additionally require an Approval-in-Principle (AiP) or risk-based/alternative-design
assessment.

\newpage

% Chapter: Prime Mover Considerations
\chapter{Prime Mover Considerations --- Diesel-Engine vs.\ Electric-Motor Driven Thrusters}
\label{ch:primemover}

\section{Purpose}

The preceding type chapters split the drive into \emph{electric} or
\emph{hydraulic}. This chapter consolidates the \textbf{additional package
elements driven by the choice of prime mover} so the surveyor can confirm the
correct drive-train documentation is present. A thruster may be driven by:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*]
\item an \textbf{electric motor} (constant- or variable-speed, converter-fed), typical of diesel-electric ships, DP vessels, cruise ships and pods; or
\item a \textbf{diesel engine} (direct/geared mechanical drive), typical of tugs, OSVs and workboats with inboard engines driving Z-drive azimuth or tunnel thrusters via shafting, clutches and gearboxes; or
\item a \textbf{hydraulic motor} (covered per type chapter and Chapter \ref{ch:hydraulics}), common on retractable and smaller units.
\end{itemize}

\section{Electric-Motor-Driven Thrusters --- Additional Submissions}

\section{Diesel-Engine-Driven Thrusters --- Additional Submissions}

Where a thruster (typically a Z-drive azimuth on a tug/OSV, or a directly driven

auxiliary systems become part of the approvable package} under
\textbf{NR467 Pt C, Ch 1, Sec 2 (Diesel Engines)} in addition to the thruster
under Sec 15. A \textbf{torsional vibration calculation (TVC) of the complete
engine--clutch--shafting--thruster system is mandatory.}

\item Crankshaft \& main running-gear data
\item Safety devices (overspeed, LO low-pressure shutdown)

\end{itemize} & NR467 Sec.2; Sec.7 \\

\end{itemize} & NR467 Sec.2; Sec.7 \\

\end{itemize} & NR467 Sec.2; Part B, Ch.5 \\
\hline

\hline

\end{itemize} & NR467 Pt C, Ch.1 \\
\hline

8 & Starting \& Control Air / Starting System & \fa{} &
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,nosep]

\end{itemize} & NR467 Pt C, Ch.1 \\
\hline

\end{longtable}

\noindent\textbf{Surveyor note (prime mover):} The single most common gap for
diesel-driven Z-drive tugs is a \textbf{missing or incomplete TVC for the whole
train} (engine + clutch + cardan/shaft + azimuth gears + propeller). For
electric drives, the recurring gaps are the \textbf{harmonic/power-quality study}
and \textbf{cable-sizing/segregation}. Hybrid PTI/PTO arrangements must document
\emph{every} driving path and the mode-changeover logic. Cross-reference the
shaft-line torsional/alignment package in Chapter \ref{ch:shafting}.

\newpage

% Chapter 5: Steering Gear
\chapter{Thrusters as Part of Steering Gear}
\label{ch:steering}

\section{Applicability}

\textbf{NR467 Pt C, Ch.\,1, Sec.\,14, [1.1.1]:} the requirements of Sec.\,14
apply to the steering gear systems of all mechanically propelled ships,
``\textbf{and to the steering mechanism of thrusters used as means of
propulsion}''.

\textbf{Sec.\,14 [4.1.1]} states which thruster types may constitute the main and
auxiliary steering gear: \textbf{azimuth thrusters, water-jets and cycloidal
propellers}, complying with Sec.\,15 as far as applicable.

\vspace{2mm}
\noindent\fcolorbox{warningred}{lightgray}{\begin{minipage}{0.95\textwidth}
\small\textbf{\textcolor{warningred}{The thruster steering package is a defined
subset --- not the whole of Sec.\,14 Table 1.}} The rule entry is
\textbf{Sec.\,14, Tab.\,1, item 13}: ``For azimuth thrusters used as steering
means: \emph{Specification and drawings of the steering mechanism}. Where
applicable, \emph{documents 2 to 6 and 8 to 12} above.'' Items 1 and 7 are
\textbf{not} pulled in. The exact subset is reproduced in Table \ref{tab:s14sub};
the additional analyses are Table \ref{tab:s14t2}; and the numeric performance
criteria a surveyor must verify are Table \ref{tab:s14crit}.

\smallskip
\noindent \textbf{Do not simply demand the full rudder steering-gear package.}
\end{minipage}}

\vspace{2mm}
\noindent The key criteria from \textbf{Sec.\,14, Article 4} (full text in
Section \ref{sec:s14art4}), which surveyors most often need at the desk:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*]
\item \textbf{Main steering:} average turning speed \textbf{$\geq$ 2,3\textdegree/s}
at maximum ahead service speed, within the declared steering angle limits;
power operated for all ships.
\item \textbf{Auxiliary steering:} \textbf{$\geq$ 0,5\textdegree/s} at half maximum
ahead service speed \emph{or 7 knots, whichever is greater}; power operated where
needed for SOLAS II-1/29.4.2 and in any ship with \textbf{$>$ 2\,500\,kW per
steering-propulsion unit}.
\item \textbf{Single unit:} two or more steering actuating systems \emph{plus a
detailed risk assessment} ([4.2.3]).
\item \textbf{Multiple units:} main $+$ auxiliary steering gear each, or two or
more identical actuating systems; and \textbf{automatic synchronisation of
thruster rotation} unless each thruster withstands the others' forces ([4.1.2]).
\item \textbf{$>$ 2\,500\,kW per unit:} alternative power supply
\textbf{automatically within 45\,s}, capacity \textbf{$\geq$ 30\,min} for ships
$\geq$ 10\,000\,GT and \textbf{$\geq$ 10\,min} otherwise ([4.2.8]).
\item \textbf{Controls} from the navigation bridge, machinery control station
\emph{and locally}, with a \textbf{thruster angle indicator independent of the
control system} at each steering control station (Sec.\,15 [2.4.1]).
\item \textbf{Water-jets as steering means will be given special consideration}
by the Society ([4.3.1]).
\end{itemize}

\vspace{2mm}
\noindent This applies to:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*]
\item Azimuth thrusters used for steering
\item Podded propulsion units providing steering
\item Water-jet systems providing steering
\item Cycloidal (Voith-Schneider) propellers used for steering
\item Any thruster configuration that replaces or supplements rudder steering
\end{itemize}

% Chapter 6: Main Shafting
\chapter{Thrusters as Part of Main Shafting}
\label{ch:shafting}

\section{Applicability}

When thruster shafts (propeller shafts, intermediate shafts, vertical shafts in azimuth thrusters, or water-jet drive shafts) are considered part of the main propulsion shafting system, they MUST comply with shafting requirements in NR467 Section 7.

This typically applies to:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*]
\item Azimuth thruster vertical and horizontal shafts
\item Podded propulsion shafts
\item Water-jet drive shafts
\item Tunnel thruster propeller shafts (for high-power units)
\item Any shaft transmitting significant propulsion power
\end{itemize}

\section{Overlap with Thruster Requirements}

\textbf{NOTE:} Many shaft-related drawings and calculations are already covered in Chapters \ref{ch:transverse}-\ref{ch:waterjets}. This chapter identifies ADDITIONAL requirements or ENHANCED detail levels required when shafts are classified as "main shafting."

\section{MON-SHAFT \& Shaft-Line Condition-Monitoring Notations}
\label{sec:monshaft}

Where the thruster forms part of the propulsion shaft line (azimuth, podded,
water-jet or high-power tunnel units with oil-lubricated stern-tube/lower-unit
bearings), the owner may request the \textbf{MON-SHAFT} additional class
notation. MON-SHAFT allows the classical periodic \emph{tailshaft withdrawal /
complete shaft survey} to be replaced by a survey regime based on
\textbf{continuous monitoring and condition assessment} of the oil-lubricated
shaft/bearing/seal arrangement. It therefore \emph{adds documentation} to the
shaft-line package and interacts directly with the thruster's lower-unit
bearing, seal and oil systems.

\begin{longtable}{|p{0.5cm}|p{5cm}|p{1.5cm}|p{5.5cm}|p{2cm}|}
\caption{MON-SHAFT --- Additional Shaft-Line Monitoring Submissions} \label{tab:monshaft} \\

\hline
\endfirsthead
\multicolumn{5}{c}%
{{\tablename\ \thetable{} -- continued from previous page}} \\
\hline
\rowcolor{bvblue}

\hline

2 & Lube-Oil Monitoring \& Analysis Programme & \fa{} &

\item Periodic oil-analysis programme (contamination, wear metals, water)
\item Sampling points and acceptance/alert criteria
\end{itemize} & NR467 Pt E (MON-SHAFT) \\
\hline

3 & Bearing Weardown / Sensor Instrumentation & \fa{} &
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,nosep]
\item Bearing temperature sensors and locations
\item Weardown/liner-wear measurement method \& trending
\item Alarm set-points and data logging
\end{itemize} & NR467 Pt E; Pt C, Ch.3 \\
\hline

4 & Survey Arrangement \& Data-Management Plan & \fic{} &
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,nosep]
\item Monitoring-based survey scheme replacing shaft withdrawal
\item Record keeping, trend review, thresholds for action
\item Interface with planned maintenance / class survey
\end{itemize} & NR467 Pt A (surveys); Pt E \\
\hline

\end{longtable}

\noindent\textbf{Surveyor note (MON-SHAFT):} MON-SHAFT is only compatible with
\textbf{oil-lubricated} shaft/bearing arrangements of an approved type. For
azimuth and podded units the ``shaft line'' extends into the lower gear/pod;
confirm the seal, bearing and oil-monitoring documentation of the thruster
lower unit (Chapters \ref{ch:azimuth}) is consistent with the MON-SHAFT scheme,
and that the oil-analysis and weardown-trending programme, sensors and
acceptance criteria are all submitted. Related shaft-line notations (e.g.\
oil-lubricated stern-tube monitoring / \texttt{TIGHT}-type arrangements) follow
the same principle --- verify the exact notation and its rule article in the
current NR467 Part E.

\newpage

% Chapter 7: Hydraulics
\chapter{Hydraulic Systems \& Pitch Control Mechanisms}
\label{ch:hydraulics}

\section{Applicability}

Hydraulic systems are used in thrusters for:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*]
\item Hydraulic motor drive (alternative to electric motor)
\item Retractable thruster lifting/lowering mechanism
\item Controllable pitch propeller pitch control
\item Azimuth slewing drive (if hydraulic)
\item Water-jet steering and reversing actuation
\item Voith-Schneider control (steering) servo system
\end{itemize}

\noindent\textbf{Rule basis:} Thruster hydraulic systems are assessed under
\textbf{NR467 Pt C, Ch 1} --- the thruster/water-jet requirements of Sec.\,15,
the \textbf{piping-system rules} (design pressure, materials, testing, relief),
and, where the hydraulics actuate a \emph{steering} function, the more stringent
\textbf{steering-gear requirements of Sec.\,14} (duplication, single-failure,
power supply, response time). Recognised standard \textbf{ISO 4413} applies to
general hydraulic-system design. Steering-critical hydraulics attract the
redundancy and control provisions of Chapter \ref{ch:steering}; pitch-control
hydraulics must define the \emph{fail-safe} pitch position.

% Due to length, I'll provide the remaining chapters in a condensed format
% Continue with Chapter 8 (DP), Chapter 9 (Testing), Chapter 10 (Checklists),
% Chapter 11 (Errors), Chapter 12 (Quick Reference), Chapter 13 (Regulatory References)

% Chapter 8: DP Thrusters (condensed)
\chapter{Dynamic Positioning (DP) Thrusters - Additional Requirements}
\label{ch:dp}

\section{Applicability}

\textbf{NR467 Sec.\,15 [1.1.1]:} ``For azimuth thrusters intended for dynamic
positioning, the additional requirements in \textbf{Pt F, Ch.\,11, Sec.\,5} are
to be complied with.'' Those requirements are \emph{in addition to} the full
type package for each thruster used in the DP system.

\vspace{2mm}
\noindent\fcolorbox{bvblue}{white}{\begin{minipage}{0.95\textwidth}
\small\textbf{The authoritative DP submission list is Table \ref{tab:dynapos}}
(NR467 Pt F, Ch.\,11, Sec.\,5, Tab.\,1 --- 22 rows, reproduced verbatim in
Section \ref{sec:dpruletable}). This chapter gives the surveyor's reading of it.
DYNAPOS [1.1.3] cross-refers back to Pt C, Ch.\,1, Sec.\,2 (engine-driven
thrusters), \textbf{Pt C, Ch.\,1, Sec.\,15} (azimuthal and transverse
thrusters) and Pt F, Chapter 3 (automation).
\end{minipage}}

\subsection{Notation symbols}
The additional class notation is \textbf{DYNAPOS}, completed by symbols
describing the capability of the installation: \textbf{AM}, \textbf{AT},
\textbf{AM/AT} and \textbf{SAM}, with the redundancy-qualified variants
\textbf{AM/AT R} and \textbf{AM/AT RS} (NR467 Pt A, Ch.\,1, Sec.\,2, [6.21.5];
Pt F, Ch.\,11, Sec.\,5). \textbf{DYNAPOS AM/AT} is equivalent to IMO equipment
class 1 ([3.2.1]). An environmental station keeping index (\textbf{ESKI}) may be
associated with the notation ([9.1.1]).

\vspace{2mm}
\noindent\fcolorbox{warningred}{lightgray}{\begin{minipage}{0.95\textwidth}
\small\textbf{\textcolor{warningred}{The FMEA is conditional --- do not demand it
as a matter of course.}} Table 1 item 17 (Failure Mode and Effect Analysis) is
annotated in the rule \textbf{``For symbols R and RS only''}, and [2.4.1]
confirms: ``For installation intended to be assigned the notation \textbf{DYNAPOS
AM/AT R} and \textbf{DYNAPOS AM/AT RS} an FMEA is to be carried out.''

\smallskip
\noindent\textbf{A plain DYNAPOS AM, AT or AM/AT installation does not attract an
FMEA under Pt F, Ch.\,11, Sec.\,5.} Earlier editions of this catalog stated
unconditionally that the FMEA is the cornerstone DP document and that an
inadequate FMEA is grounds for rejection. That is correct \emph{for R and RS
installations} and wrong for the rest. Check the requested notation string first.

\smallskip
\noindent Note the related worst-case-failure provision: for \textbf{AM/AT R} or
\textbf{AM/AT RS}, the DP vessel is to be operated so that the worst-case failure
determined in [2.4.1] can occur at any time without breaching the acceptable
excursion criteria ([3.1.1]). For \textbf{AM/AT}, the power system need not be
redundant ([4.2.4]).
\end{minipage}}

\section{Thruster-specific rows in the DYNAPOS table}
Three rows bear directly on the thruster package and are easily overlooked:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*]
\item \textbf{Item 11 (\fa{})} --- for approval of propulsion based on rotary
azimuth thrusters: \emph{layout drawings of thrust units, thrust shafts and
blocks; arrangement of hull passages; thrust curves of each propulsion unit.}
\item \textbf{Item 18 (\fa{})} --- \emph{study of possible interaction between
thrusters.}
\item \textbf{Item 3 (\fa{})} --- \emph{capability plots}, covering the
conditions in the specification, the case with all thrusters running and
selected in DP, \emph{and the worst case failure}.
\end{itemize}

\section{Reference}
\textbf{NR467 Part F, Chapter 11, Section 5} (DYNAPOS) is the governing text;
DP control system type testing follows \textbf{Pt C, Ch.\,3, Sec.\,6} (item 10).
See also IMO MSC.1/Circ.1580 and IMCA guidance, and \textbf{NR632} for
hardware-in-the-loop testing of DP control systems.

\newpage

% Chapter: Ice Class
\chapter{Ice Class \& Cold-Climate (Winterisation) Requirements}
\label{ch:ice}

\section{Applicability --- and the scope split that matters most}

\noindent\fcolorbox{warningred}{lightgray}{\begin{minipage}{0.95\textwidth}
\small\textbf{\textcolor{warningred}{Read this before demanding anything in this
chapter.}} The ice scope depends entirely on the thruster's \textbf{duty}.
\textbf{NR467 Sec.\,15 [1.1.1], verbatim:}

\smallskip
\noindent ``Thrusters intended for \textbf{propulsion and steering} of ships with
an ICE CLASS notation are to comply with the additional requirements of Part F,
Chapter 8. \textbf{Transverse thrusters intended for manoeuvring} of ships with
an ICE CLASS notation \textbf{are required to comply with additional requirement
Pt F, Ch.\,8, Sec.\,3, [3.5.1] only}.''

\smallskip
\noindent And \textbf{[3.5.1] in its entirety} reads: \textit{``Tunnels of
transverse thrusters are to be fitted with grids for protection against ice
impacts.''}

\smallskip
\noindent\textbf{So for a manoeuvring bow or stern tunnel thruster on an
ice-class ship, the entire ice requirement is a protective grid on the tunnel.}
The ice-load strengthening package in this chapter applies to
\emph{propulsion and steering} thrusters. Applying it to a manoeuvring tunnel
unit is a substantial over-demand and a common review error.
\end{minipage}}

\vspace{3mm}
\noindent The notations, per \textbf{NR467 Pt F, Ch.\,8, Sec.\,1, [1.1.1]}, are:
\textbf{ICE CLASS IA SUPER, ICE CLASS IA, ICE CLASS IB, ICE CLASS IC, ICE CLASS
ID, YOUNG ICE 1} and \textbf{YOUNG ICE 2}. Per [1.1.2] the strengthening
requirements --- \emph{excepting} ICE CLASS ID, YOUNG ICE 1 and YOUNG ICE 2 ---
are equivalent to the \textbf{Finnish-Swedish Ice Class Rules 2019, as amended},
for ships trading in the Baltic in winter or equivalent ice conditions.

\vspace{2mm}
\noindent\textbf{Polar service.} Thrusters intended for propulsion and steering
of ships with an additional class notation \textbf{POLAR CLASS} or a service
notation \textbf{icebreaker} comply with \textbf{NR527} (Sec.\,15 [1.1.1]).
Where the vessel has an \textbf{azimuth} propulsion system, the controlling
document is \textbf{NR584} --- see Section \ref{sec:nr584}. Machinery ice loads
follow \textbf{IACS UR I3}, whose propulsion machinery type factor is
$k_3 = 1{,}0$ for fixed and $1{,}2$ for azimuthing propulsors.

\vspace{2mm}
\noindent Cold-climate service without ice loads is covered by winterisation
notations such as \textbf{COLD}, addressing design ambient temperatures rather
than ice impact (NR467 Pt F, Ch.\,8, Sec.\,4).

\section{Ice-Strengthening Submissions}

\noindent\textbf{Applies to thrusters intended for \emph{propulsion and
steering} only} --- see the scope box above. The authoritative sub-article map
is Table \ref{tab:iceart1}; the table below is the supporting engineering
content. For an azimuth unit, note especially Pt F, Ch.\,8, Sec.\,3,
\textbf{[1.11]} --- thruster body ice loads, global vibration, \textbf{steering
gear design torque in ice} and protection of the steering gear against
excessive torque.

\begin{longtable}{|p{0.5cm}|p{5cm}|p{1.5cm}|p{5.5cm}|p{2cm}|}
\caption{Ice Class --- Additional Thruster Requirements} \label{tab:ice} \\
\hline
\rowcolor{bvblue}
\textcolor{white}{\textbf{\#}} & \textcolor{white}{\textbf{Drawing/Document}} & \textcolor{white}{\textbf{Category}} & \textcolor{white}{\textbf{Content Requirements}} & \textcolor{white}{\textbf{Reference}} \\
\hline
\endfirsthead
\multicolumn{5}{c}%
{{\tablename\ \thetable{} -- continued from previous page}} \\
\hline
\rowcolor{bvblue}
\textcolor{white}{\textbf{\#}} & \textcolor{white}{\textbf{Drawing/Document}} & \textcolor{white}{\textbf{Category}} & \textcolor{white}{\textbf{Content Requirements}} & \textcolor{white}{\textbf{Reference}} \\
\hline
\endhead
\hline \multicolumn{5}{|r|}{{Continued on next page}} \\ \hline
\endfoot
\hline
\endlastfoot

1 & Ice Class Notation Basis \& Design Conditions & \fa{} &
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,nosep]
\item Declared notation (IA Super/IA/\ldots\ or PC1--PC7)
\item Design ice thickness / ice-load basis
\item Applicable rule edition and lower design temperature
\end{itemize} & NR467 Part F; NR527 \\
\hline

2 & Propeller Ice-Load Strength Calculation & \fa{} &
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,nosep]
\item Ice interaction loads (milling, impact) on blades
\item Blade root and blade tip strength vs.\ ice bending
\item Blade material and thickness increase
\item Open vs.\ ducted / nozzle ice loads
\end{itemize} & NR467 Part E; IACS UR I3 \\
\hline

3 & Shaft Line \& Ice Torque Verification & \fa{} &
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,nosep]
\item Maximum ice-induced torque and thrust
\item Propeller shaft, intermediate shaft, couplings
\item Overload/peak-torque capacity of gears
\item Blade-loss / one-blade-milling scenarios
\end{itemize} & NR467 Part E; Sec.7; IACS UR I3 \\
\hline

4 & Gear \& Azimuth/Nozzle Ice-Load Substantiation & \fa{} &
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,nosep]
\item Bevel/reduction gears vs.\ ice peak torque
\item Azimuth slewing bearing/gear vs.\ ice-block impact on lower unit
\item Nozzle/steering-grid ice-impact strength
\end{itemize} & NR467 Part E; Sec.15 \\
\hline

5 & Thruster Body / Lower Unit Ice Protection & \fa{} &
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,nosep]
\item Lower gearbox/pod shape vs.\ ice interaction
\item Ice knife / deflector where required
\item Local structural strengthening
\end{itemize} & NR467 Part E \\
\hline

6 & Tunnel / Inlet Ice \& Grid Arrangement & \fa{} &
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,nosep]
\item Tunnel thruster grid ice/debris protection
\item De-icing / anti-blocking provisions
\item Ice-load on grid bars
\end{itemize} & NR467 Part E; Sec.15 \\
\hline

7 & Winterisation of Systems (Hydraulic, Cooling, Seals) & \fa{} &
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,nosep]
\item Low-temperature grades of oils/greases/seals
\item Heating/insulation of exposed hydraulics \& sensors
\item Sea-chest/cooling anti-icing (recirculation)
\item Materials for low-temperature toughness (Charpy)
\end{itemize} & NR467 Part E (COLD); Part B, Ch.3 \\
\hline

8 & Materials Low-Temperature Certification & \fa{} &
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,nosep]
\item Impact testing at design temperature
\item Grade selection for exposed \& load-bearing parts
\end{itemize} & NR467 Part B, Ch.3; Part E \\
\hline

9 & Retraction/Deployment in Ice (if retractable) & \fic{} &
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,nosep]
\item Operating limits and procedures in ice
\item Protection of opening/seals from ice ingress
\end{itemize} & NR467 Part E \\
\hline

\end{longtable}

\noindent\textbf{Surveyor note (ice):} The governing case for ice-classed
propulsion is usually the \textbf{ice peak torque / blade-milling load}
propagating through propeller $\rightarrow$ shaft $\rightarrow$ gear
$\rightarrow$ prime mover, \emph{not} the open-water rating. Verify the ice-load
calculation explicitly and check that it is carried consistently into the shaft
(Chapter \ref{ch:shafting}) and gear strength checks. For azimuth/podded units in
ice, confirm lower-unit and slewing-gear substantiation against ice-block impact
--- a frequent omission. Match the rule edition to the declared notation.
